\subsection{31. The code audit of 2026-09-29: findings checked and applied}\label{31-the-code-audit-of-2026-09-29-findings-checked-and-applied}

\texttt{md/CODE\_AUDIT\_20260929.md} (Codex, with its probes in
\texttt{docs/audit\_20260905/code\_audit\_20260929/}). Each finding was checked
against the source before anything was changed. The audit document was made
ASCII (curly quotes and dashes); its disposition section records the same.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{F2, the admissibility predicate accepted infinities: FIXED.}
  \texttt{UW\_conversions.f90}, \texttt{positive\_density\_and\_internal\_energy}: +Inf density
  or energy compares true against zero and passed. Every conserved component
  and the internal energy must now be finite. The audit\textquotesingle s probe
  (\texttt{guard\_probe.f90}, linked against the fixed objects) now reads
  finite accepted, +Inf energy refused, +Inf density refused, NaN refused.
  A finite state takes the same branch as before, so no output moves.
\item
  \textbf{F3, a steady route wrote a state without certifying it: FIXED.} The
  direct \texttt{EXHALE\_PTC=1} route wrote whatever certificate the solve had left;
  it now calls the same final certification as the stationary restart
  (\texttt{certify\_the\_state\_to\_be\_written}, one routine for both: the claim, the
  boundary rebuilt from the written composition, the residual assembled from
  that state, the certification evaluated on it). And a solve whose root
  stays on the other side of its own contact rule after the second solve
  (section 30.4) now returns info = 2 instead of 0. The restart route calls
  the same statements in the same order as before.
\item
  \textbf{F4, the transit tool replaced non-finite transmission by 1 or 0:
  FIXED.} \texttt{EXHALE\_transit.py}, \texttt{\_checked\_transmission}: a non-finite value
  or a departure from {[}0, 1{]} beyond 1e-12 stops the synthesis and names the
  first wavelength; the rounding of the Ly-alpha disk average is still
  clipped (the only line that was clipped before), and He 10830, H-alpha
  and H-beta are checked without clipping. The comment that exp(-tau)
  overflows in a saturated wing was wrong (it underflows) and is gone.
  MEASURED on the molecular K\_zz = 1e9 He/H 2.13 catalog output: every
  number of the five \texttt{tpm\_*} files is identical before and after.
\item
  \textbf{F5, the equivalent-width operator: FIXED.}
  \texttt{LHS1140b/he10830\_equivalent\_width.py}: the model curve is refused when
  it is not one-dimensional, not of one length, not finite (a NaN
  wavelength used to pass) or not strictly ordered, and a transit file
  with a non-finite column or a non-positive continuum; the observational
  error now uses the trapezoidal weights of the estimator itself.
  MEASURED: EW 1.1084293096 \%A unchanged, error 0.0294775 -\textgreater{} 0.0288956 \%A
  (0.030 -\textgreater{} 0.029 to two figures). The error is ours, computed from the
  released spectrum under independent samples, not a number of Cherubim et
  al. (2026).
\item
  \textbf{F7, a changed link library did not relink: FIXED.} \texttt{Makefile}: a
  second stamp whose name hashes the link command (compiler, flags,
  \texttt{LDLIBS}); the executables depend on it. MEASURED: \texttt{make\ -q} with an
  unchanged command returns 0, with \texttt{LDLIBS=-l\_exhale\_missing\_library} it
  returns 1, and \texttt{make\ -n} then shows the link alone, no compilation.
\item
  \textbf{F8, the formation-energy diagnostic proposed a double count:
  CORRECTED (text).} \texttt{src/utils/formation\_energy\_flux\_diagnostic.py}
  said the sensible-energy equation lacks -div(F\_form) and a chemical
  source; in the sensible-energy form the chemical conversion is already
  the reaction heating and cooling (h nu - I for a photoionization), and
  adding either term counts it twice. The header now states the correct
  balance and what the script measures (the chemical share of the energy
  ledger); no computation changed.
\item
  \textbf{F1, the sensible enthalpy carried by the diffusing carriers is not in
  the energy equation: CONFIRMED, MEASURED, OPEN.} The carrier transport
  (\texttt{diffusive\_photochemistry.f90}) moves H2 relative to the gas and returns
  no energy flux; \texttt{interdiffusion\_enthalpy\_active} requires He diffusion and
  covers the elements only, so an H2 counterflow at zero hydrogen flux
  carries q = (h\_H2 - 2 h\_H) Phi\_H2 that nothing represents. DERIVED on two
  certified states (well-mixed He/H 0.19 and K\_zz = 1e9 He/H 0.65), from the
  face diffusive fluxes of the carrier row terms and h\_H2 - 2 h\_H =
  k (u\_rv - 5T/2) with a rigid rotor and a harmonic vibrator (0.4 per cent
  from the code\textquotesingle s own H2 caloric at 1000 K): \textbar div q\textbar{} is 1 to 3 per cent of
  the heating over the molecular layer (median 1.8 per cent over
  1.001 \textless{} r \textless{} 1.03), 8 to 11 per cent at the base cell, and as large as the
  net heating or larger where heating and cooling cancel; the integral of
  \textbar div q\textbar{} is 1.4 to 1.5 per cent of the integrated heating, while the SIGNED
  integral vanishes (-1e-18 of it), as the closed diffusive boundary of the
  carrier operator requires (script, inputs with their md5 and the numbers:
  \texttt{docs/audit\_20260905/code\_audit\_20260929\_F1/}).
  It can move the temperature of the H2 layer; the implementation (the
  carrier face fluxes into the energy rows of the steady residual and the
  marching update, the compensating atomic flux from the element
  constraints, tests) is the next item.
\item
  \textbf{F6, the pass snapshot is two renames, not one publication: OPEN, a
  decision.} A reader-interface and data-layout change (a state generation
  with a manifest and one published pointer); left for the user\textquotesingle s decision.
\item
  Sections 11 to 13 of the audit (the wind-only certification scope, the
  abundance mask of absent carriers, trace caloric approximations, general
  EOS Riemann tests, the outer diffusive closure, parallel tests,
  optimization order) are statements of scope and proposals, recorded in
  \texttt{md/TO\_BE\_DONE.md}; none is a defect to fix in place.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{32. The follow-up review of the audit corrections (2026-09-29)}\label{32-the-follow-up-review-of-the-audit-corrections-2026-09-29}

\texttt{md/CODE\_AUDIT\_20260929\_review1.md} (Codex; probes in
\texttt{docs/audit\_20260905/code\_audit\_20260929\_recheck/}) confirmed F2, F4, F5 and
F7 and the substance of F3 and F8, and named four remaining points. All four
were checked in the source and applied.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{R1, the direct steady route stopped with status 0 whatever the final
  certificate said.} It now ends with \texttt{certification\_stop\_uncertified},
  the exit contract of the other steady routes: a stationary claim the final
  certificate refuses exits 2 after the state and the resolved configuration
  are written; an unfinished snapshot exits 0.
\item
  \textbf{R2, the formation-energy diagnostic described more than it computes.}
  It computes the advective part u\_form v only; the header now says so,
  gives the stationary identity with the relative transport term, lists what
  a difference contains (omitted relative transport, the species residual of
  the saved state, missing species, the flux reconstruction), and states the
  total energy balance of an open, gravitating wind with its work and
  boundary terms instead of "only radiation".
\item
  \textbf{R3, the admissibility test could overflow before rejecting.} The sign
  of rho e is now taken as the sign of 2 rho E - (rho v)\^{}2 on the components
  divided by the largest of them, so nothing can overflow
  (\texttt{admissible\_conserved\_cell}, one definition used by the stage test and by
  the positivity repair, whose own cell test had accepted infinities as
  well). Under -ffpe-trap=overflow,invalid the 1e160 cell of the review is
  admitted (rho e = 5e159) with no trap, and its negative-energy variant, a
  tiny-density large-momentum cell and a negative-energy ordinary cell are
  refused. The three marching stages now test the interior before
  \texttt{Apply\_BC} builds the boundary on it: \texttt{Apply\_BC} reads the physical cells
  and writes only the ghosts, and neither the test nor the repair reads the
  ghosts of the trial, so the decisions are unchanged and an inadmissible
  interior no longer reaches the boundary and EOS routines.
\item
  \textbf{R4, the transmission messages quoted metres as Angstrom.} The
  wavelength arrays of \texttt{EXHALE\_transit.py} are in metres; the messages now
  quote 1e10 times them, and the function states its unit.
\item
  \textbf{The F1 estimate} is now kept as a script with its inputs and the
  signed and absolute integrals (section 31 corrected: the quoted 1.5 per
  cent was the integral of \textbar div q\textbar; the signed integral vanishes).
\end{itemize}

Checks of this section (MEASURED): the overflow probe above; the audit\textquotesingle s
admissibility probe (finite accepted, +Inf and NaN refused); the six
regression cases that march or take the stationary restart route
(\texttt{hp\_zero\_seed}, \texttt{hp\_trace\_seed}, \texttt{hp\_front}, \texttt{hydrostatic\_column},
\texttt{oxygen\_chemistry}, \texttt{iontrans\_metals}) bitwise identical to their goldens
with REGRESSION\_REL\_TOL=0 (gfortran \texttt{EXHALE.x} 67c12cd1); the transmission
function on a NaN and on a rounding excursion. The production ifx build of
this source is \texttt{EXHALE\_64954795.x} (manifest
\texttt{BINARY\_MANIFEST\_64954795cb2a.txt}), built with \texttt{make\ -B} on lart2 because
\texttt{/nfs/mocafe} is mounted with a 30-minute attribute cache there and a plain
\texttt{make} did not see the sources edited on lart4 (it relinked the old
objects; the md5 stayed that of the previous build).

Two findings of the runs of this morning, recorded here and in
\texttt{md/TO\_BE\_DONE.md} (m): the well-mixed chain (\texttt{wm10}, the contact held)
certified 0.3 to 0.1 and stopped at 0.09, where every hydrodynamic solve
converged and the H2 carrier row of the OUTERMOST cell (500, r = 30 R\_p) grew
slowly pass after pass (0.09: 1.2e-3 to 2.5e-3; 0.09487: 3.5e-3 to 4.0e-3)
with every carrier relaxation ending where the carriers stopped moving,
the symptom the 2026-09-24 memo recorded at He/H 0.083 and a candidate for
the zero diffusive carrier flux at the outer face (audit section 12.3); and
the four reduced-XUV atomic He/H 9.7 cases fail their first solves with the
contact held, so their obstacle is not the contact threshold.

\subsection{33. The state of every pass published as one generation (audit F6)}\label{33-the-state-of-every-pass-published-as-one-generation-audit-f6}

Audit finding F6 (\texttt{md/CODE\_AUDIT\_20260929.md} section 8,
\texttt{md/CODE\_AUDIT\_20260929\_review1.md} section 9): the stationary route wrote
the state of every outer pass as \texttt{output/Hydro\_ioniz\_last\_pass.txt} and
\texttt{output/Ion\_species\_last\_pass.txt}, each half through its own \texttt{.part} file
and its own rename. An interruption between the two renames left halves of
two passes, and the pass number of \texttt{cert\_reason=pass\_snapshot\_p\textless{}n\textgreater{}} did not
tell two runs apart (two runs both write pass 1), so a mixed pair from two
runs with compatible metadata was not excluded. Changed with the user\textquotesingle s
approval.

What changed (\texttt{src/modules/files\_IO/write\_output.f90},
\texttt{src/modules/files\_IO/load\_IC.f90}, \texttt{src/modules/functions/utilities.f90},
\texttt{src/EXHALE\_main.f90}):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{A run identity.} \texttt{establish\_run\_identity}, called once after \texttt{init},
  forms \texttt{run\_identity} = \texttt{yyyymmddThhmmss.sss\_c\textless{}count\textgreater{}} from
  \texttt{date\_and\_time} (to the millisecond) and the 64-bit \texttt{system\_clock} count,
  both standard Fortran; characters outside {[}A-Za-z0-9.\_-{]} are replaced.
\item
  \textbf{One generation for each pass.} \texttt{publish\_pass\_state\_generation}
  (it replaces \texttt{write\_pass\_state\_pair}) writes, with the writers and header
  lines the pair had, \texttt{Hydro\_ioniz.txt}, \texttt{Ion\_species.txt} and
  \texttt{manifest.txt} into \texttt{output/pass\_state/.\textless{}gen\textgreater{}.tmp/}, with
  \texttt{\textless{}gen\textgreater{}\ =\ r\textless{}run\_identity\textgreater{}\_p\textless{}pass\textgreater{}}. The manifest states, one \texttt{key=value} a
  line, \texttt{state\_id}, \texttt{run\_identity}, \texttt{pass}, \texttt{previous\_state\_id}, the two file
  names with their sizes in bytes (INQUIRE \texttt{size=}), \texttt{certified=F} and
  \texttt{cert\_reason=pass\_snapshot\_p\textless{}pass\textgreater{}}.
\item
  \textbf{The order of publication.} (1) the three files are written and closed
  in the temporary directory; (2) \texttt{rename(2)} of that directory onto
  \texttt{output/pass\_state/\textless{}gen\textgreater{}}; (3) \texttt{output/pass\_state/current.part} is written
  with \texttt{\textless{}gen\textgreater{}}, closed and renamed onto \texttt{output/pass\_state/current}, the one
  rename that publishes; (4) the generation \texttt{current} named before is kept,
  and the one it had replaced (its manifest\textquotesingle s \texttt{previous\_state\_id}) is removed
  file by file under its explicit name and then \texttt{remove(3)}d as an empty
  directory. Directories are made with a C \texttt{mkdir} binding (mode 0755) and
  removed with C \texttt{remove}, beside the existing \texttt{rename} binding; no shell
  command and no pattern is used. A name read from a file is removed only if
  it starts with \texttt{r} and has only {[}A-Za-z0-9.\_-{]} characters.
\item
  \textbf{An identity in both halves.} The coupling line of both halves of a
  generation ends in \texttt{state\_id=\textless{}gen\textgreater{}} (\texttt{write\_coupling\_state\_header} takes
  it as an optional argument; no other file carries it). \texttt{load\_IC} reads it
  with the certification pair (\texttt{parse\_certification\_claim}) and
  \texttt{adopt\_certification\_claim} refuses a pair whose halves state two
  different ids, and a pair in which one half states an id and the other
  none. A pair in which neither states one (every final state, every older
  file) loads exactly as before; a pair that does prints the generation.
\item
  \textbf{A measurement key.} \texttt{EXHALE\_PASS\_STATE\_STOP\_AT=\textless{}step\textgreater{}{[}:\textless{}pass\textgreater{}{]}} stops
  the run with \texttt{stop\ 3} right after step 1, 2 or 3 of the publication of that
  pass (of every pass if none is named). Unset: no effect.
\end{itemize}

What changes for the user: the files moved from
\texttt{output/\{Hydro\_ioniz,Ion\_species\}\_last\_pass.txt} to
\texttt{output/pass\_state/\textless{}gen\textgreater{}/\{Hydro\_ioniz,Ion\_species\}.txt}, and the generation
to take is the one \texttt{output/pass\_state/current} names; the old names are no
longer written. The final \texttt{Hydro\_ioniz.txt} / \texttt{Ion\_species.txt} of a run are
unchanged (no \texttt{state\_id}). An interruption before step 2 leaves a
\texttt{.\textless{}gen\textgreater{}.tmp} directory, one between steps 2 and 3 a complete generation no
pointer names; neither is read and neither is removed by a later run. The
continuation script of the LHS 1140 b rungs is being executed by running
jobs, so it was not edited in place: the new version is
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/s2\_continuation/config\_rung.sh.new}, to be moved onto
\texttt{config\_rung.sh} when no process uses it. Its entry precedence is (a) the
parent\textquotesingle s final pair, (b) the generation \texttt{current} names, read once and taken
only if its three files exist and the pointer, the manifest\textquotesingle s \texttt{state\_id} and
both headers\textquotesingle{} \texttt{state\_id} agree, otherwise refused, (c) a legacy
\texttt{*\_last\_pass.txt} pair with the checks it had. \texttt{run\_check.sh} clears
\texttt{output/*.txt} of a case before a run and leaves \texttt{output/pass\_state/}, which
holds at most two generations and is not compared.
\texttt{src/tests/steady\_selfconsistent\_residual/run.sh} never read the pair.
Documents updated: \texttt{README.md}, \texttt{README\_HOWTO.md}, \texttt{md/input\_schema.md}
appendix D, \texttt{docs/EXHALE\_user\_manual.tex} (section "pass\_state/", label
\texttt{sec:passstate}, and the key in the table of environment variables),
\texttt{docs/physics\_overview/steady\_state.tex}, \texttt{boundaries\_initial.tex},
\texttt{postprocessing.tex}; both PDFs rebuilt without errors or undefined
references.

Checks (MEASURED 2026-09-29 on lart4, gfortran 16.2, binary built in its own
object directory \texttt{build\_f6}, md5 \texttt{b8cf7a21f2fa1149b1d9131cafbd44a0}; the
tree also held other uncommitted edits in progress at that moment):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Publication and retention.} \texttt{iontrans\_metals} inputs and IC in a

  and 3 published; afterwards \texttt{current} named \texttt{...\_p3}, \texttt{...\_p2} was kept,

  byte sizes (94446, 448089) equal the files, and the manifest and both

  The regression run of \texttt{iontrans\_metals} (below) published 14 passes and
  left exactly \texttt{...\_p13} and \texttt{...\_p14}, \texttt{current} naming \texttt{...\_p14}.
\item
  \textbf{Interruption.} Three runs started within 30 ms of one another, each on a

  \texttt{EXHALE\_PASS\_STATE\_STOP\_AT=k:2}, all exiting with status 3, got three

  \texttt{...228.522\_c...722562}). k = 1: \texttt{current} named the run\textquotesingle s complete \texttt{p1};
  \texttt{.\textless{}p2\textgreater{}.tmp/} held its three files. k = 2: \texttt{current} named \texttt{p1}; \texttt{p2} was
  complete and unnamed. k = 3: \texttt{current} named the complete \texttt{p2}; \texttt{p1} and
  the previous run\textquotesingle s \texttt{p3} were still present (step 4 not reached). At every
  point \texttt{current} named a generation whose three files existed and whose
  manifest and headers stated its name. The previous run\textquotesingle s \texttt{p2} had been

\item
  \textbf{load\_IC.} Hydro of \texttt{p2} with Ion of \texttt{p3}: refused, exit 1, both ids

\item
  \textbf{config\_rung.sh.new}, with \texttt{BIN=/bin/true} on dummy parents: a final

\subsection{34. The enthalpy the transported carriers carry (audit F1), and the regression comparison of residual columns (2026-09-29)}\label{34-the-enthalpy-the-transported-carriers-carry-audit-f1-and-the-regression-comparison-of-residual-columns-2026-09-29}

\textbf{The physics.} A molecular carrier (H2; OH, H2O, CO with the oxygen
chemistry) is moved by the carrier operator relative to the rest of its own
elements: its diffusive, eddy and settling number flux Phi\_c moves nuclei
that the element fluxes do not, and the non-carrier species of the same
elements move the other way, nucleus for nucleus. Cook (2009, eqs. 11-13)
then puts q\_car = sum\_c Phi\_c {[}h\_c - sum\_el nu\_\{c,el\} hbar\_el{]} into the
energy equation, h\_c the enthalpy of one carrier particle and hbar\_el the
enthalpy of the non-carrier species of element el per nucleus, every
particle with the electrons its charge gave up, as in
\texttt{component\_specific\_enthalpies}; an H2 molecule rising through atomic
hydrogen at zero hydrogen flux carries k (u\_rv - 5T/2), about -1.5 kT at
1000 K. Before this section the energy equation carried the element part
only (section 29.3), which does not see a carrier moving within its element.

\textbf{The code.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{diffusive\_photochemistry.f90}: \texttt{carrier\_enthalpy\_face\_flux} (the flux at
  every face from the carrier operator\textquotesingle s own coefficients and face rule,
  \texttt{carrier\_face\_coefficients} and \texttt{carrier\_face\_flux}, with the temperature
  and the particle count of the state passed, so it follows the iterate of a
  steady solve), \texttt{carrier\_enthalpy\_divergence\_of\_state} (cells 1 ... N on the
  carrier rows\textquotesingle{} geometry; both ends of the carrier column are closed, so the
  column sum is zero) and \texttt{carrier\_enthalpy\_active}. Writes no module state.
\item
  The stationary energy row (\texttt{steady\_residual.f90}, \texttt{assemble\_residual})
  adds the divergence into \texttt{Sidf}, the one record of the enthalpy of relative
  transport. The residual module cannot use the carrier module (which uses
  it), so the carrier module registers the procedure in a pointer of the
  residual module when the carrier set is decided (\texttt{carrier\_set\_init}); a run
  without the term leaves it null.
\item
  The marching step (\texttt{EXHALE\_main.f90}) subtracts dt div q\_car after an
  accepted carrier step, from the fluxes of the composition that step
  returned (the step\textquotesingle s own fluxes to first order in dt, exactly at a fixed
  point, so the two routes solve one energy equation).
\item
  One key for both terms, \texttt{Interdiffusion\ enthalpy\ flux:} (default True): it
  now also acts with \texttt{Molecular\ carrier\ transport:\ True}. A new restart token
  \texttt{carrier\_enth} (absent in older files, read as F), so a restart from a
  state written without the term names it on a \texttt{Restart\ option\ change:}
  line.
\item
  Not covered: the ionization stages of \texttt{Ionization\ transport} (their face
  flux contains the element\textquotesingle s advection; the relative part is not formed),
  recorded in \texttt{md/TO\_BE\_DONE.md} (j).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Checks (MEASURED).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  New suite \texttt{src/tests/carrier\_enthalpy\_flux/} (10 rows, all PASS) on a
  synthetic HI/H2 column at zero hydrogen flux: both end faces carry
  nothing; the column sum of V div q is 4e-17 of the sum of \textbar V div q\textbar; the
  face flux equals Phi\_H2 k (u\_rv - 5T/2), Phi\_H2 recomputed from the
  operator\textquotesingle s coefficients, to 3e-15; the key off and a run without carrier
  transport give exactly zero.
\item
  The certified well-mixed He/H 0.19 state (\texttt{wm10\_0.19\_check}) reloaded with
  the term (\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/audit\_F1/wm019\_F1}, gfortran b029a1e8): the
  code\textquotesingle s divergence, from the conservation budget of pass 1, is a median
  1.84 per cent of the heating over 1.001 \textless{} r/R\_p \textless{} 1.03 (the independent
  estimate of section 31: 1.83), 1.6 per cent in absolute integral (1.5),
  zero in signed integral, largest 9.9 per cent at r = 7.2 R\_p. That state is
  not stationary with the term: the gated carrier row stood at 7.1e-3 on
  the first pass and fell by about 18 per cent a pass (1.7e-4 at pass 20),
  every hydrodynamic solve converging. So every molecular state certified
  before this section is a state of the previous energy equation.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The regression comparison of residual columns} (user decision of
2026-09-29). \texttt{backup/regression/compare\_within\_tolerance.py} measured every
entry against itself, so a residual column that is rounding at a solution
read 0 against 3.1e-15 as a relative difference of 1 (\texttt{adv\_mass\_row} of
\texttt{wasp\_full\_newton}, section 30.4). A column whose header name ends in \texttt{\_row}
is now measured against the largest value of its golden column; every other
column keeps the value-by-value measure (a state column spans decades, and
a column-wide scale would hide a change of its small values). The flagged
file now reads within 2.5e-9.

\textbf{What the term changes (MEASURED).} The He/H 0.19 reload above certified
again after 35 passes (\texttt{certified\_in\_wind}). Against the state certified
without the term: T higher by 8 to 19 K in the H2 layer (r \textless{} 1.01 R\_p),
lower by a median 4.8 K over 1.3-3 R\_p and by at most 24 K (r = 4.0 R\_p,
1774 -\textgreater{} 1749 K); the wind-window mass flux -0.55 per cent; the He 2\^{}3S
density higher by a median 1.9 per cent over 1.05-5 R\_p, at most 6.3 per
cent. The equivalent width was not measured (the stationary route writes no
\texttt{\_adv} pair); a change of the order of the He 2\^{}3S change, about 2 per cent,
is DERIVED, against an observational error of 2.6 per cent. Figure:
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/audit\_F1/wm019\_before\_after\_F1.pdf}.

\textbf{The regression matrix and the goldens (MEASURED 2026-09-29).} The full
matrix on the F1 + F6 source: twelve cases bitwise or within 1e-3; the four
cases with \texttt{Molecular\ carrier\ transport:\ True} differ as F1 makes them
(\texttt{mol\_carrier}, \texttt{hp\_zero\_seed}, \texttt{hp\_trace\_seed}, \texttt{hp\_front}); and
\texttt{oxygen\_chemistry} wrote nothing, because its IC predates the \texttt{carrier\_enth}
token: its input now names \texttt{Restart\ option\ change:\ carrier\_enth}, and it is
then bitwise its golden, since in that 1000-step cold transient the carrier
interval is never covered (1049 of 1049 step attempts) and the carriers never
move, so F1 adds nothing by construction. The goldens of the four changed
cases were re-snapshotted (the previous ones kept in
\texttt{backup/regression/golden\_pre\_F1\_20260929/}), with \texttt{iontrans\_metals} so that
its reference directory is current, and the four re-checked bitwise PASS.

\subsection{35. Why the mapped atomic He/H 9.7 seeds at reduced XUV do not solve (2026-09-29, open)}\label{35-why-the-mapped-atomic-heh-97-seeds-at-reduced-xuv-do-not-solve-2026-09-29-open}

The four atomic He/H 9.7 cases at 0.01, 0.06 and 0.0624 of the fiducial XUV
(\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/atomic97/}) were moved from the catalog states onto the
face-at-level grid (\texttt{facelevel\_continue.sh}, section 29.11) and restarted
stationary. The hydrodynamic solve ended \texttt{info=2} on every pass. Measured on
the 0.06 case, \texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/atomic97/diag\_R/} (one directory for each
measurement, \texttt{Restart\ intent:\ stationary\ evaluate} unless named otherwise):

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
{\small
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/4\relax}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/4\relax}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/4\relax}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/4\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
run & state and binary & residual norm & largest row \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
D1 & catalog state, previous grid, \texttt{EXHALE\_69233669.x} (the binary that wrote it) & 5.6e-7 & the catalog value \\
D2 & the same state, \texttt{EXHALE\_7db5afe9.x} (2026-09-26, previous grid) & 5.7e-2 & energy, r = 1.072 \\
D4 / D5 & mapped seed, \texttt{EXHALE\_6dd54955.x}, element enthalpy flux off / on & 1.88 / 1.88 & mass, cell 13 (all of it below r = 1.03) \\
D6 & mapped seed, \texttt{EXHALE\_a7255a03.x} (the first face-at-level binary) & 1.88 & as D4 \\
D9 & D4 with v below r = 1.003 set to the wind flux / (rho r\^{}2) & 1.86 & mass, cell 13 \\
D10 & D4 with ln rho, ln p and T below r = 1.03 interpolated as cubics & 1.82 & mass, cell 110 (where the two interpolations meet) \\
D11 & D4 with 50, 110 or 200 cells rebuilt in the discrete well-balanced equilibrium & 1.54 & mass, cell 6 \\
D13 / D14 & D11 (110 cells) with cell 1 anchored to the reservoir pressure, without / with the smooth velocity of D9 & 1.54 / 1.18 & mass, cell 6 / cell 1 \\
\end{longtable}
}
}

What this shows, and what it does not:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The seed is a wind: its mass-flux spread above r = 1.03 is 0.32 per cent,
  the same as the catalog state\textquotesingle s. The spread of 0.42 to 0.48 in the logs of
  the stationary restarts is what the first JFNK solve left (27 to 42
  non-monotone accepts), not the seed.
\item
  CORRECTED after the review (35.1): the two bullets written here first
  read D9 to D14 from the maximum over all cells and excluded the
  interpolation of the lower layer and the reconstruction. That was wrong.
  The element enthalpy flux is not the direct cause of the fixed-state mass
  maximum (D4 = D5 in the mass row), and nothing more is excluded.
\item
  The heating of the lower layer at fixed T is higher by about 8 per cent
  under the face-at-level binary than under \texttt{EXHALE\_7db5afe9.x} at the same
  radius (1.139e-9 against 1.053e-9 erg cm\^{}-3 s\^{}-1 at r = 1.010); the
  increase is the charge-exchange channel (35.1), while cooling is below 2
  per cent of heating from the base to 1.1 R\_p in both.
\item
  A marching relaxation does not settle the seed: 5000 steps take the
  mass-flux spread from 0.003 to 3.8 and back to 1.9, and 41000 steps (D8)
  leave it at 1.09 to 1.10 from step 15000 on. That relaxation ran its first
  stage with PLM (\texttt{Restart\ intent:\ relaxation} enters the two-stage
  PLM -\textgreater{} WENO3 marching), while the state and every stationary solve above
  are WENO3.
\item
  \texttt{Restart\ intent:\ relaxation} does not relax a seed whose spread is below
  the Newton switch (1e-2): a restart arms the hand-off at load
  (\texttt{du\_newton\_armed\ =\ do\_load\_IC}), and the 0.06 seed was handed to JFNK at
  marching step 2.
\end{itemize}

The two 0.01 cases were already refused in the catalog with the same
signature (flat mass flux, mass and energy rows of order 1 in the first
cells); the 0.06 and 0.0624 cases acquired it with the grid and code change.
The whole account, with the next steps considered, is
\texttt{md/atomic\_heh97\_reduced\_xuv\_20260929.md}. The four runs are stopped (their
\texttt{EXIT.txt} say why). The same failure, a
first JFNK stalled at \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} 1.32 on the mass row of cell 2, stopped the F1
re-solve of the mapped \texttt{m7\_HeH1.45} state; that He/H is instead being reached
from \texttt{kzz\_1.223\_v4b}, a state solved on the face-at-level grid
(\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/audit\_F1/launch\_F1\_kzz\_1.223\_to\_1.45.sh}).

\subsubsection{35.1 The review of the account (2026-09-29)}\label{351-the-review-of-the-account-2026-09-29}

\texttt{md/atomic\_heh97\_reduced\_xuv\_20260929\_review.md} (its retained evidence is
the directory \texttt{atomic\_heh97\_review\_20260929/} of \texttt{docs/audit\_20260905/}: a
diagnostic linked to
the production objects, built with bounds checks, reproducing the production
face fluxes exactly) was checked here and taken over:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The continuity defect is a numerical face flux: on the reconstructed
  traces of the mapped seed the Roe pressure-jump term (-Delta p\_WB / 2c,
  Delta p\_WB about 1e-7 of p at Mach 4e-8) reverses the mass flux at face 12
  and removes most of it at face 13, and at face 0, whose two traces differ by
  4.9e-4 of the pressure, carries about 1e4 times the wind flux inward. The
  low-Mach velocity-jump correction scales Delta v and leaves this (1.876).
\item
  A lower layer projected into the reconstruction\textquotesingle s discrete equilibrium
  with the momentum set to the wind flux takes cell 13 from 1.876 to 1.3e-4;
  cell 1 stays at 1.18 and the join at cell 111 rises to 1.50 (READ from the
  retained results). The interior is repaired; the base boundary and the
  join are not.
\item
  The base boundary continues cell 1 along a hydrostatic isentrope, the
  reconstruction balances each cell with a constant-density equilibrium, and
  \texttt{Rec\_BC} keeps the interior trace of face 0: three constructions, not one
  discrete equilibrium. The comment in \texttt{base\_boundary.f90}
  (\texttt{characteristic\_base\_face\_state}) that claimed the boundary exactly
  balanced in any hydrostatic atmosphere at any resolution was corrected
  (comment only; no binary change beyond line numbers).
\item
  The heating difference is the nonradiative He+ + H -\textgreater{} He + H+ channel,
  8.785e-11 against zero at r = 1.010 (\texttt{Heating\_breakdown.txt} of D2 and D4,
  MEASURED here as well): the rate of section 25, 9.6e-17 cm\^{}3 s\^{}-1 at
  1057 K against 2.8e-21 before, whose threshold continuation carries a band
  of 1.5e-5 to 3.6 times at 1e3 K. A deliberate physics change, not a defect;
  a sensitivity test moves the forward rate, its reverse and the heat
  together. Heating cannot make the fixed-state continuity defect.
\item
  The first JFNK solve cut the certification distance from 7.96e10 to
  1.19e9 and the mass row from 1.88 to 0.099 while spoiling the outer wind; a
  failed multi-scale solve, not by itself evidence against the non-monotone
  acceptance.
\end{itemize}

The next steps in the review\textquotesingle s order (a face-flux budget diagnostic,
discrete equilibrium tests of the boundary with the reconstruction, one
discrete equilibrium at the base, a seed that satisfies the lower-layer
equations, continuation, and only then the nonlinear formulation) are listed
in \texttt{md/atomic\_heh97\_reduced\_xuv\_20260929.md} section 5; none is started, and
the base boundary change needs its design agreed first.

\subsection{36. The face-flux budget export and the discrete equilibrium tests of the base (2026-09-29)}\label{36-the-face-flux-budget-export-and-the-discrete-equilibrium-tests-of-the-base-2026-09-29}

Steps 1 and 2 of \texttt{md/atomic\_heh97\_reduced\_xuv\_20260929\_review.md} section 8.
No equation, flux, source or boundary changed.

\textbf{The face-flux budget export (schema 3).} \texttt{EXHALE\_CONSERVATION\_BUDGET=\textless{}n\textgreater{}}
(\texttt{src/modules/time\_step/conservation\_budget.f90}, called from
\texttt{assemble\_residual} in \texttt{steady\_residual.f90}) now appends to the 42 columns
of schema 2, for the upper face of each row: the reconstructed left and right
traces (rho, v, p) the flux was formed from, \texttt{wb\_dp\_eq} and the two
departures, the pressure jump Delta p\_WB the Roe flux uses, the central mass
transport of the physical flux, the pressure-jump part of the numerical mass
flux (production \texttt{Num\_flux} with the jump minus the same call with the jump
set to zero: an attribution, not a flux), and the recomputed numerical mass
flux; for each cell, the absolute and normalized continuity residual (the
scale the stationary solve divides by) and the signed cumulative mass budget.
A header block states the lower boundary of the assembly: the reservoir, the
interior state continued to the face along cell 1\textquotesingle s isentrope (recomputed
from the inputs the boundary used and checked bitwise against the values it
stored), the characteristic face velocity, the two traces of face 0 and the
numerical base flux. Columns that a branch does not define are NaN, with the
reason in the header. The flux branch counters the extra \texttt{Num\_flux} calls
would move are saved and restored. MEASURED: on the review\textquotesingle s 0.06 input the
new columns reproduce the review\textquotesingle s table (face 0: +3.28224e-4, 9995.84 times
the wind flux; faces 12 and 13: pressure-jump parts -4.05157e-8 and
-2.59041e-8 against central transport 3.28e-8), the continuity rows
telescope from the exported face fluxes with a difference of 0, and the
state files are the same with the export on and off (data identical; only
the provenance time differs); \texttt{hydrostatic\_column} with the export off is
bitwise the binary before the change. The base block of that input shows the
two interior pressures of face 0 that do not agree: the isentropic
continuation the boundary compares with the reservoir, 0.999877 p0, and the
reconstructed interior trace the flux sees, 0.999509 p0.

\textbf{The discrete equilibrium tests.} Two measurement programs in
\texttt{src/tests/grid\_and\_gates/} (\texttt{base\_reservoir\_discrete\_equilibrium.f90},
\texttt{low\_mach\_face\_mass\_flux.f90}, a shared module \texttt{base\_equilibrium\_columns.f90},
input \texttt{base\_reservoir\_equilibrium.inp}; \texttt{run.sh} names
\texttt{base\_reservoir\_equilibrium} and \texttt{low\_mach\_face\_flux}), each on a ladder of
three lower cell widths (2e-4 x 50, 1e-4 x 100, 5e-5 x 200 R\_p), production
\texttt{Apply\_BC}, \texttt{Reconstruct}, \texttt{Num\_flux} and \texttt{assemble\_residual} with no
sources. Columns: \texttt{a\_iso}, \texttt{a\_warm} built exactly in the reconstruction\textquotesingle s
discrete constant-density equilibrium (wb\_dp\_eq = 0) and anchored to the
reservoir pressure at face 0; \texttt{b\_iso} an analytic isothermal column; \texttt{c\_isen}
an analytic isentrope. MEASURED (16 PASS, 37 FAIL, the failures being the
findings; the README marks both tests RED by design):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The well-balanced interior keeps its discrete equilibrium to rounding
  (interior momentum rows 1.2e-14 and 4.6e-14 of their scale on \texttt{a\_iso},
  \texttt{a\_warm}). The base does not: on \texttt{a\_iso} the face-0 flux is -1.46e-4 of
  (rho c)\_res, v\_b = -2.6e-4 c\_res, the ghost-0 / cell-1 mismatch is
  wb\_dp\_eq(0) = 2.9e-4 p, and the cell-1 momentum row is 3.1e-3 of its scale.
\item
  These are truncation errors: the face fluxes converge at order 2.0, the
  cell-1 momentum row at order 1.0, on every column.
\item
  Against a manufactured wind at Mach M they grow as 1/M: on \texttt{a\_iso} the
  face-0 flux misses the wind by -0.136 at M = 1e-3, -14.6 at 1e-5 and -1.47e4
  at 1e-8 (1.47e4, 3.61e3, 8.93e2 on the three grids), while the interior
  faces 2-10 stay within 5.3e-4 at every M. On \texttt{b\_iso} (continuum but not
  discrete balance) the interior faces are off by 1.03 at M = 1e-8 as well.
\end{itemize}

Interpretation (not measured as such): two mechanisms act at face 0. The
outgoing relation takes p\_i from the isentropic continuation of cell 1, which
differs from cell 1\textquotesingle s own constant-density equilibrium at the face by about
(1/2 gamma)(dr/2H)\^{}2 (4.3e-4 here) and drives v\_b; and the reconstructed
right trace departs from p\_res because WENO3 forms its departure from ghost
0, which lies on the reservoir isentrope and not on cell 1\textquotesingle s equilibrium.
Refinement cannot bring the base flux to 1 per cent of a Mach 1e-8 wind (a
base cell about 300 times narrower than the finest tested would be needed);
the boundary has to share the reconstruction\textquotesingle s discrete equilibrium (review
step 3), and a seed has to be projected onto it (step 4).

Also corrected: the header of \texttt{hydrostatic\_residual.f90} named a SAVEd
\texttt{tol\ =\ 1.0} in \texttt{define\_grid} as the reason one grid is built in one process;
\texttt{define\_grid} now sets \texttt{tol} before each search, and the reason is the
operator\textquotesingle s module arrays allocated at the first N. The grid-and-gates README
count ("Twenty-one tests") and its list of names were stale and were
corrected.

\subsection{37. One discrete equilibrium at the base (2026-09-29)}\label{37-one-discrete-equilibrium-at-the-base-2026-09-29}

Step 3 of \texttt{md/atomic\_heh97\_reduced\_xuv\_20260929\_review.md} section 8. Under
\texttt{Well\ balanced:\ True} the base condition reads the interior pressure at the
level face as cell 1\textquotesingle s own constant-density equilibrium,
p\_i = P\_dn(1) = p\_1 + rho\_1 (phi\_c(1) - phi\_i(0)), the pressure the
reconstruction extrapolates cell 1 to (\texttt{base\_boundary.f90},
\texttt{interior\_state\_at\_level\_face}), in place of cell 1\textquotesingle s hydrostatic isentrope.
The two ghosts continue the same equilibrium below the level,
P\_up(0) = p\_b and P\_up(-1) = P\_dn(0), at the reservoir-side temperature
(\texttt{base\_ghost\_averages}). Once the contact rule has chosen the face density
rho\_b, the face velocity carries the interior\textquotesingle s mass flux at that density
(v\_b = v\_i rho\_i/rho\_b + (p\_b - p\_i)/(rho\_i c\_i)): a stationary face with
flow through it has no contact. The reservoir still states the pressure and
entropy of inflowing gas; the equilibrium shared is mechanical, not thermal
(Kaeppeli \& Mishra 2016, A\&A 587, A94, section 2.1). Without the option the
previous construction is unchanged. The model identity is
\texttt{...\_face\_at\_level\_discrete\_equilibrium\_v5} with the option and the
unchanged \texttt{...\_face\_at\_level\_v4} without it (\texttt{base\_boundary\_model\_id}).

MEASURED (the tests of section 36): on columns built in the discrete
equilibrium, v\_b, the face-0 flux and the base momentum rows went from
2.6e-4 c, 1.46e-4 rho c and 3.1e-3 of the weight to below 1e-14; the base
face carries a manufactured wind of Mach 1e-3 to 1e-9 to 2.1e-3 (isothermal
column) and 2.5e-3 (warming column), first order in the cell width, where
the previous boundary missed it by 1.47e4 at Mach 1e-8. Carrying the flux at
rho\_1 instead of rho\_b would have left 2.8 and 6.7 per cent; a temperature
contact at the level with flux through it is not a stationary flow and was
moved to a static-only test column. Analytic columns (continuum, not
discrete, balance) now show their O((dr/H)\^{}2) offset at the base face
(order 2.0). grid\_and\_gates: 361/37 -\textgreater{} 393/11 PASS/FAIL, the eleven all the
analytic isothermal column. On the mapped atomic 0.06 seed the cell-1 row
went 1.378 -\textgreater{} 1.206 and the face-0 flux 1.0e4 -\textgreater{} 2.2e4 times the wind: the
seed\textquotesingle s cell 1 lies 5.5e-4 of p off the discrete equilibrium, which the
isentrope had partly offset; the seed must be projected (section 38). Two
continuation states certified under v4 (K\_zz = 1e9 and well-mixed He/H
0.55, with the carrier enthalpy flux) re-certified under v5 in 2 and 4
passes, starting at \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} 1.14 and 1.13. Also corrected: the v2 identity in
\texttt{md/input\_schema.md}, the v3 literal in
\texttt{src/tests/boundary\_state/ghost\_composition\_fixed\_point.sh}, the missing
\texttt{carrier\_enth} token in \texttt{molecular\_seed\_of\_an\_atomic\_state.sh}.

\subsection{38. The atomic He/H 9.7 cases: the seed projection, and the element enthalpy flux as the remaining obstacle (2026-09-29)}\label{38-the-atomic-heh-97-cases-the-seed-projection-and-the-element-enthalpy-flux-as-the-remaining-obstacle-2026-09-29}

\textbf{The seed projection.} Measurement key \texttt{EXHALE\_SEED\_HYDROSTATIC\_CELLS=K}
(\texttt{src/modules/init/init.f90}, \texttt{seed\_layer\_in\_discrete\_equilibrium}, off when
unset, requires \texttt{Well\ balanced:\ True}): after load\_IC, cell 1 is set so that
its constant-density equilibrium reaches the reservoir pressure at the level
face, cells 2 to K so that wb\_dp\_eq of faces 1 to K-1 vanishes, every cell
above K (upper ghosts included) is scaled by the one factor that balances
face K, all with T and the mass fractions of every cell held; the momentum
of cells 1 to K carries the mass flux of cell K+1 and above K the velocity
is divided by the density factor. MEASURED on the mapped 0.06 seed under v5
(\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/atomic97/diag\_R/P\textless{}K\textgreater{}\_v5\_projected}): mass and momentum
rows 1.88 and 5.0e-3 -\textgreater{} 0.133 and 3.2e-5 (K = 50), 3.6e-2 and 8.5e-6 (110),
3.7e-3 and 7.6e-7 (200 and 300); deeper than local Mach 1e-3 the neglected
momentum flux shows (1.5e-5 at K = 400). The energy row stays at 1.05: it is
not projected.

\textbf{What the energy row is.} From the budget export of the projected seed
(cell, dF\_energy, heat - cool, Sidf): cell 50, 8.6e-6, 9.4e-6, +1.44e-5;
cell 200, 4.3e-6, 4.6e-6, +5.4e-6; cell 2, 5.0e-6, 3.0e-5, -5.4e-4. Without
Sidf the energy row would be about 8 per cent of the heating from cell 3 up;
the element interdiffusion enthalpy flux (section 29.3), absent when the
seeds were solved, is 1.1 to 1.5 times the heating over 1.0 - 1.15 R\_p. It is
physical: helium stays while hydrogen escapes, and the escaping hydrogen
carries (5/2) kT per particle relative to the barycentric velocity, an
enthalpy flux of the order of the wind\textquotesingle s own. Cell 2\textquotesingle s spike is the helium
diffusive flux through face 1, 2.8 times that of the faces above, because
cell 2\textquotesingle s helium fraction is about 1e-6 off the diffusive steady state
against the reservoir composition of cell 1; the seed\textquotesingle s composition is not
in its element steady state (the catalog refused these states on the
elemental He/H row). With the term off, cell 1 still reads 0.94: the heat of
the base cell, which the previous boundary\textquotesingle s spurious face fluxes had been
carrying away.

\textbf{Solves.} From the projected seeds with the term on (\texttt{*\_v5proj},
\texttt{EXHALE\_710ab9ca.x}), the hydrodynamic solve returned info = 2 on every
pass with the mass and momentum rows unchanged, the energy row grew 1.09 -\textgreater{}
1.40 over the element steps, and 0.06, 0.0624 and the photochemical 0.01
were refused by the progress control at pass 4 (the scalar 0.01 was stopped
in its second pass). With the term off (\texttt{*\_v5proj\_enthoff}), the same seeds
solve: hydro info = 0 on pass 1 in 83 s, \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} 5.1e-8 (0.06) and 4.8e-8
(0.0624); the elemental He/H row, 2.2e-2 and 1.1e-2 at pass 1, moves between
5.7e-3 and 1.4e-2 over passes 2 to 5, then falls by about a factor 2 a pass,
and both states CERTIFY at pass 24 (He/H row 8.5e-6 and 9.9e-6 against
1e-5; run logs of \texttt{*\_v5proj\_enthoff}). A marching test of the
projected 0.06 seed (20000 steps, term on) settles at a mass-flux spread of
1.09 to 1.10, as before the boundary change.

\textbf{The continuation factor.} Measurement key
\texttt{EXHALE\_INTERDIFF\_ENTH\_SCALE=s}, 0 \textless= s \textless= 1, 1 when unset
(\texttt{binary\_element\_diffusion.f90}, \texttt{interdiffusion\_enthalpy\_divergence}, the
one place both the stationary row and the marching update take the term
from): the divergence is multiplied by s, so a state of the full equation
(s = 1) can be reached from one solved without the term in steps. A state at
0 \textless{} s \textless{} 1 is a step of that continuation, not a prediction.

checked against the code before anything was changed; the verdicts:

\textbf{Checks} (ifx and gfortran builds of the edited tree; only the paths the
edits touch). \texttt{src/tests/interdiffusion\_enthalpy\_flux/run.sh}: 18 PASS. Gate
tests \texttt{low\_mach\_face\_flux\ output\_state\ restart\_round\_trip\ restart\_option\_change\ restart\_intent\ base\_reservoir\_equilibrium}: 120 PASS,

those states are continuation steps and are not published. State files
written before 2026-09-29 by ifx builds carry 15-digit values; they are

\subsection{40. Why the atomic He/H 9.7 continuation fails: two mechanisms, and the heat conduction the models omit (2026-09-30, open)}\label{40-why-the-atomic-heh-97-continuation-fails-two-mechanisms-and-the-heat-conduction-the-models-omit-2026-09-30-open}

Diagnosis of the failed rungs of section 39, read from their run logs
(\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/atomic97/*\_adNN\_*}, \texttt{*\_xd01\_*}); every number
MEASURED.

\textbf{A. The alternation between the hydrodynamic solve and the element step
diverges} (0.06, s = 0.174 to 0.2375). Each hydrodynamic solve converges
(info = 0, quadratic in its last iterations, lam = 1, the linear solve at its
tolerance in 1 to 6 products), but the elemental He/H row grows from pass to
pass and the under-relaxation of the element step does not stop it; the
hydrodynamic solve fails only once the composition has moved far:

\textbar{} s = 0.195, pass \textbar{} hydro start \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} \textbar{} hydro \textbar{} He/H row \textbar{} omega \textbar{}
\textbar-\/-\/-\textbar-\/-\/-\textbar-\/-\/-\textbar-\/-\/-\textbar-\/-\/-\textbar{}
\textbar{} 1 \textbar{} 0.092 \textbar{} info = 0 \textbar{} 9.0e-3 \textbar{} 0.5 \textbar{}
\textbar{} 2 \textbar{} 0.17 \textbar{} info = 0 \textbar{} 2.3e-2 \textbar{} 0.5 \textbar{}
\textbar{} 3 \textbar{} 0.32 \textbar{} info = 0 \textbar{} 1.4e-1 \textbar{} 0.25 \textbar{}
\textbar{} 4 \textbar{} 0.72 \textbar{} info = 0 \textbar{} 3.8e-1 \textbar{} 0.125 \textbar{}
\textbar{} 5 \textbar{} 0.39 \textbar{} info = 2 \textbar{} 3.5e-1 \textbar{} 0.125 \textbar{}

The rung at s = 0.174 that the chain accepted ended at pass 14 with the
He/H row at 0.51 (hydro info = 0): the acceptance on \texttt{hydro\ info=0} alone
(section 39, R5) carried an unconverged composition into every later rung.
The last states of the 0.06 chain that are converged are s = 0.125
(certified) and s = 0.153 (He/H row 1.05e-4, not certified); the chain
stopped at 01:07 with its step below 0.005.

\textbf{B. The stationary solve does not move from its start} (0.0624 from
s = 0.125 to 0.2375; the XUV step 0.06 -\textgreater{} 0.05 at s = 0). The composition
converges at the held hydrodynamics (He/H row 9.9e-6 -\textgreater{} 2.7e-8 over 12
passes), but every pass starts the solve at the same \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} (0.2671; 0.183)
and ends there. Within a solve the pseudo-time steps are accepted at
lam = 1 while dtau doubles; near dtau \textasciitilde{} 2e3 a step overshoots (\textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{}
0.06 -\textgreater{} 0.24 -\textgreater{} 0.28, the ionization root of 1 to 2 cells outside the
simplex in the trial evaluations), dtau is cut, \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} returns to 0.053 while
the fixed-scale merit rises, and after 40 iterations without improvement of
either functional the solve stops and returns its best judged iterate,
which is the entry state (every move raises the continuity row against its
3e-12 tolerance). The linear model is not the obstacle (the cycle reaches
its tolerance in one product); the path the pseudo-transient has to take is
longer than the solve\textquotesingle s stagnation window.

\textbf{The energy equation of these models omits heat conduction} (\texttt{cond=F},
the default). Evaluated on the stored states with the conductivity the code
carries (\texttt{viscous\_conduction.f90}: Watson, Donahue \& Walker 1981 neutral
atomic hydrogen, 4.45e4 (T/1000 K)\^{}0.7 erg cm\^{}-1 s\^{}-1 K\^{}-1),
\textbar(1/r\^{}2) d/dr(r\^{}2 kappa dT/dr)\textbar{} against the local heating, and the
conductive flux F\_c against the advected enthalpy flux of the wind
(\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/atomic97/conduction\_vs\_heating.py\ \textless{}Hydro\_ioniz.txt\textgreater{}},
from the columns of the state file and R0 of its grid line):

(\textbar conduction\textbar{} / heating.) On the 0.06 state F\_c is 1.5 to 3.5 erg cm\^{}-2 s\^{}-1
from the base to 1.3 R\_p against an advected enthalpy flux of 0.014 to 0.15;
on the fiducial atomic He/H 9.7 state 2 to 3.4 against 0.3 to 2.8. The
lower layers of the LHS 1140 b catalog are therefore solved without a term
of the energy equation that is of order one and, at reduced XUV, one to two
orders above the heating. \emph{Likely} this is also why the reduced-XUV models
are so stiff: without conduction the temperature of a layer whose wind runs
at Mach 1e-8 is set by the heating divided by the mass flux, and the
element enthalpy flux, of the order of the wind\textquotesingle s own, moves it by a large
factor (section 38). Not established: whether the temperature structure
with conduction differs by as much as the ratio suggests; the published
models that include conduction (Koskinen et al. 2013a, 2022; Yelle 2004)
state it as small for their hot-Jupiter cases, whose base wind is 2.5 km/s
(Koskinen 2022) and not Mach 1e-8.

\textbf{Conductivity of a helium-dominated gas.} The code applies the atomic
hydrogen value whatever the composition. Yelle (2004), eqs. 10-11, forms
the neutral conductivity of a mixture from the constituents\textquotesingle{} values
(Banks \& Kockarts 1973) with a Wilke-type rule; at He/H = 9.7 the helium
value decides it. Banks \& Kockarts (1973) is not in \texttt{references/}; the value
is not adopted until it is read in the original.

\textbf{Test.} From the certified 0.06 term-off state, \texttt{Conduction:\ True}
switched on at once (binary \texttt{EXHALE\_a1c14b9c.x}, tree source of section 39;
\texttt{*\_cond\_enthoff}, \texttt{*\_cond\_enthon} with the element enthalpy flux on at
s = 1): the loaded \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} is 1.00 and 1.84 (the conduction term alone), and
the first stationary solve of each STAGNATED after 90 iterations and
returned the entry state. A state with conduction is not reached from one
without it in one solve either; both runs were stopped after their first
pass.

Open, for the user: whether conduction becomes part of the LHS 1140 b model
(it changes every model of the catalog), with which conductivity for the
helium-rich mixtures, and by which route the states with it are reached (a
continuation factor on kappa as on the element enthalpy flux, or time
marching with the implicit conduction update).

\subsection{41. Heat conduction with the conductivity of the mixture, and its continuation factor (2026-09-30)}\label{41-heat-conduction-with-the-conductivity-of-the-mixture-and-its-continuation-factor-2026-09-30}

User decision of 2026-09-30: heat conduction enters the LHS 1140 b model,
reached by continuation in the conductivity; the no-conduction runs were
stopped (the 0.0624 s chain had ended at s = 0.181, the XUV descent was
stopped at x = 0.0573, rung 4; the scalar 0.01 run had been refused at
pass 20).

\textbf{The conductivity} (\texttt{viscous\_conduction.f90}, section 2, Eq. 4\textquotesingle), read in
the published versions:

\begin{verbatim}
kappa = (1/n) [ n_e 1.2e-6 T^2.5 + n_HI 379 T^0.69 + n_HeI 299 T^0.69 ]   erg cm^-1 s^-1 K^-1
\end{verbatim}

Salz et al. (2015, A\&A 576, A21), section 3.4.2, Eqs. 24-26 (electron-ion,
Spitzer; neutral hydrogen; the number-weighted sum), and Sutton et al.
(2015, JGR 120, 6884), Eq. 5 (the helium coefficient 299 T\^{}0.69 in the same
weighted form); both quote Banks \& Kockarts (1973), which was not available.
n counts every particle (electrons, atoms, ions, metals); the ions add
nothing of their own (Yelle 2004, Eq. 9, gives 7.4e-8 T\^{}2.5/sqrt(mu), below
1e-2 of kappa\_H at 1e4 K). The previous coefficient, Watson et al. (1981)
4.45e4 (T/1000 K)\^{}0.7 (verified in Erkaev et al. 2016, Eq. A6), was the
neutral hydrogen value applied to any composition; the hydrogen term now is
Salz Eq. 25, within 2 per cent of it from 300 to 7000 K (1.013 at 300 K,
1.0006 at 1000 K, 0.981 at 7000 K). On the
0.06 state the mixture coefficient is 0.81 of kappa\_H where helium is 90
per cent of the particles. The viscosity keeps the hydrogen value (5); it
is not used by any LHS 1140 b case. None of the three sources gives an H2
coefficient; the H2 term is n\_H2 k\_H2(T) with k\_H2 the tabulated conductivity
of Incropera et al. (2007, Fundamentals of Heat and Mass Transfer, 6th ed.,
Table A.4), 100 to 2000 K, linear in (ln T, ln k) and continued outside with
the exponent of the end interval (an extrapolation), \texttt{h2\_conductivity}
(the user supplied the book, 2026-09-30; Holman, Heat Transfer, 10th ed.,
Fig. 1-4, shows H2 and He only up to 500 C). The same table gives helium
0.152 and 0.354 W m\^{}-1 K\^{}-1 at 300 and 1000 K, against 299 T\^{}0.69 = 1.53e4
and 3.51e4 erg cm\^{}-1 s\^{}-1 K\^{}-1: the helium coefficient of Sutton et al.
agrees to 1 per cent. (A first version of this entry stopped molecular runs
with conduction; that stop is removed.) The table was first interpolated in
(ln T, ln k); at the user\textquotesingle s request (2026-09-30) it is replaced by a fitted
function, kappa\_H2 = A T\^{}s {[}1 + c E(theta\_v/T){]}, E(x) = x\^{}2 e\^{}x/(e\^{}x - 1)\^{}2,
A = 272.523, s = 0.735652, c = 0.370446, theta\_v = 5987 K (the v = 0 -\textgreater{} 1
spacing of the Roueff et al. 2019 ladder the code carries): the Eucken form,
fitted over 200-2000 K, largest deviation 2.5 per cent (200 K), rms 1.05 per
cent; valid 200-2000 K, an extrapolation outside (+7.6 per cent at 150 K,
+20 per cent at 100 K where rotation freezes out). Other forms measured on
the same table: a power law 6 per cent, Sutherland 2 to 12 per cent, a cubic
in ln T 1 per cent (untrustworthy outside). The fit has a continuous
derivative (the interpolation kinked at 25 nodes); above 2000 K it lies 3
and 6 per cent below the end-slope continuation at 3000 and 4000 K, neither
being a measurement. Linked probe: 200/300/1000/2000 K give 1.025, 0.989,
1.013, 0.986 of the table. No run with molecules and conduction has been
made with either form. Yelle (2004) Eq. 10, the Wilke-type mixture rule,
was not used: as printed it is not dimensionally the Wilke form (a factor
1/r in the sum), \emph{likely} a typesetting error.

\textbf{Code.} \texttt{thermal\_conductivity(Tcell,\ f\_sp,\ kap)} forms the number fractions
from the composition of the same cells (calc\_ne, calc\_ntot: the equation of
state\textquotesingle s own counts), and the composition is passed down
\texttt{thermal\_conduction\_coeffs}, \texttt{thermal\_conduction\_source},
\texttt{viscous\_conduction\_sources} (steady residual) and \texttt{viscous\_conduction\_step}
(marching); the test driver \texttt{src/tests/energy\_update/conduction\_floor\_tests.f90}
passes a neutral-hydrogen composition. Continuation factor
\texttt{EXHALE\_CONDUCTION\_SCALE\ =\ s} (\texttt{conduction\_scale()}), 1 when unset; the
\texttt{cond} token of the options field carries s wherever s /= 1, so a restart
across a change of s names \texttt{cond} (the rule of section 39, R1); the input
echo and the setup report state the coefficient and the factor.

\textbf{Checks.} gfortran and ifx builds; \texttt{src/tests/energy\_update/run.sh}: 54 PASS,
0 FAIL. \texttt{Restart\ intent:\ stationary\ evaluate} of the certified 0.06 term-off
state with \texttt{Conduction:\ True} and the budget export: the conduction term of
the energy row (Sene, viscosity off) over the heating agrees with an
independent evaluation of Eq. 4\textquotesingle{} from the written columns to five digits at
every radius tested (-46.04 at 1.003, +41.40 at 1.10, -34.07 at 1.20, +12.42
at 3.0 R\_p).

\textbf{Continuation, first attempt} (\texttt{.P1/atomic97/factor\_continuation\_chain.sh},
new: a rung is accepted only when certified, the chain ends successfully only
at s = 1 certified with exit 0, every rung names its token; binary
\texttt{EXHALE\_0c5924c7.x}): from the certified term-off 0.06 and 0.0624 states, the
first rung s = 0.005 returns its entry state from every hydrodynamic solve
(hydro info = 2, \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} 1.000 at the energy row of cell 1). The row of cell 1
is dominated by the conduction through the base face, where the operator
holds the reservoir\textquotesingle s temperature (Dirichlet, \texttt{conduction\_base\_level\_T}) and
the no-conduction states carry a large temperature jump across half a
2e-4 R\_p cell; since the row is measured against its own largest term, its
relative residual stays at 1 for any s. Both chains were stopped at pass 6
of rung 1. A smaller factor cannot help; the obstacle is mechanism B of
section 40.

\textbf{Mechanism B measured} (\texttt{.P1/atomic97/diag\_R/B1\_jump\_scan\_cond0.005},
\texttt{EXHALE\_RESID\_JUMP\_SCAN=1} at the entry state of that rung): along the first
Krylov direction the residual is smooth from 2 arcs down to 3e-5 arcs of the
probe step; below that the only steps are rounding (the largest moves its
row by 4e-7 of it, 0.6 to 1.7 times the arithmetic bound epsilon x face state
/ width). A discontinuous residual is therefore not what rejects the steps
of lam 1e-6 at dtau 8e-5. At such dtau the step is dY \textasciitilde{} -dtau F, the explicit
pseudo-time step, and its rejection means the merit (the scaled 2-norm of F)
rises along the physical relaxation direction. \emph{Likely} this is the
obstacle: the pseudo-transient continuation is globalized by a merit line
search, which can refuse a relaxation that is stable in time but not
monotone in that norm. Kelley \& Keyes (1998, SIAM J. Numer. Anal. 35, 508),
abstract: "Standard globalization strategies such as line search or trust
region methods often stagnate at local minima. Ψtc succeeds in many of these
cases by taking advantage of the underlying PDE structure of the problem";
their pseudo-time step is set by the residual reduction (SER, their Eq. 1.5),
and they report a nonmonotonic steady-state residual norm at the outset of
the iterations for iterates far from the steady state. Changing the acceptance
of the solve is a solver change and is held for the user (the stage 2 solver
program is closed, 2026-09-10).

\subsection{42. Pseudo-transient steps accepted by the pseudo-time control (2026-09-30)}\label{42-pseudo-transient-steps-accepted-by-the-pseudo-time-control-2026-09-30}

User decision of 2026-09-30: the acceptance of the pseudo-transient solve
may be changed (the stage 2 solver program is reopened for this item).

\textbf{What was changed} (\texttt{steady\_newton.f90}, \texttt{solve\_steady\_jfnk}; measurement
key, off by default, so no solve without it moves). \texttt{EXHALE\_PTC\_SER=1\textbar{}2}:
a trial of the line search is accepted when it is admissible (the existing
\texttt{admissible} test of \texttt{eval\_residual}: positive, finite, a chemical root in
every cell) and no cell\textquotesingle s density or total energy moves by more than
\texttt{EXHALE\_PTC\_SER\_MAX\_CHANGE} (default 0.3) of itself; otherwise lam is halved.
No merit descent is asked. The returns to the best iterate after 20
iterations without improvement, the stagnation stop after 40, the return
after a run of damped steps, and the damped Gauss-Newton escape replacing
an accepted step are not taken; the solve ends at its gates, at its
iteration cap or after 12 consecutive iterations without an admissible
trial, and hands back its LAST iterate unless the best one met the gates
(its iterates are a relaxation path, not a search). The pseudo-time: \texttt{=1},
SER (Kelley \& Keyes 1998, Eq. 1.5), dtau \textless- dtau min(2, max(1/2,
f2/f2\_try)); \texttt{=2}, doubled on a full step; both times lam on a shortened
step. The outer alternation adopts the returned state whatever info is
(\texttt{EXHALE\_main}, after \texttt{solve\_steady\_jfnk}), so progress carries over.

\textbf{Measured} (\texttt{.P1/atomic97/diag\_R/SER/}, one outer pass, cap 400
iterations, binaries \texttt{EXHALE\_d0d5bf27.x} (=1) and \texttt{EXHALE\_9d0b8d4c.x} (=2);
every fixture returned its entry state under the merit line search):

leaves the elemental He/H row at 0.23: mechanism A (the diverging hydro /

continuation chains meet with their certified-only acceptance.

\subsection{43. The continuation chains, first three hours, and their step control (2026-09-30)}\label{43-the-continuation-chains-first-three-hours-and-their-step-control-2026-09-30}

With \texttt{EXHALE\_PTC\_SER=2} (section 42) every conduction rung below s \textasciitilde{} 0.14
certified in 7 to 29 passes (5 to 15 minutes): 0.06 up to s = 0.0673,
0.0624 up to 0.137, the photochemical 0.10 up to 0.0673 (its term-off
state had certified first, in 9 passes, where the merit line search had
refused it). Two defects of the chain script were measured and fixed
(\texttt{.P1/atomic97/factor\_continuation\_chain.sh}):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the step ratio grew as q\^{}1.5 on every success, so 0.06 jumped from 0.0673
  to 0.698 (x10.4) and the photochemical case to 0.32; a rung that fails
  under \texttt{EXHALE\_PTC\_SER=2} runs to the solve\textquotesingle s iteration cap (3000, the
  stagnation stop being off there), 2 h 20 min for the 0.06 rung (hydro
  info = 2, elemental row 0.89). Now the ratio is capped (\texttt{Q\_CAP}, 2) and
  every solve of a chain carries \texttt{EXHALE\_JFNK\_MAXIT=400} (the certified
  solves take 22 to 28 iterations);
\item
  0.0624 was refused twice at 30 passes with the elemental row at 1.35e-5
  and 1.07e-5 against 1e-5, i.e. still converging; a refused rung whose
  hydrodynamic solve converged and whose elemental row is below \texttt{NEAR}
  (1e-4) is now continued once at the same factor from its own state before
  the step is shortened.
  The factor at which the alternation of 0.0624 diverges (mechanism A) is
  between 0.137 (certified) and 0.225 (refused, elemental row 7.8e-2 at pass
  5). The chains were stopped at 06:35 and restarted from their last
  certified rungs (\texttt{S\_START}) by one wrapper for all three cases,
  \texttt{.P1/atomic97/launch\_full\_equation\_from.sh} (conduction to 1, then the
  element enthalpy flux, then for 0.06 and the photochemical case the XUV
  descent to 0.01).
\end{itemize}

The \texttt{cond} and \texttt{interdiff\_enth} tokens carry the factor in F7.5, five
decimals: two factors that differ below 1e-5 carry one token.

The thermosphere is nearly isothermal at 900-940 K from 1.05 to 5 R\_p (the
XUV heat conducted down to the 226 K reservoir), in place of a 7290 K peak
at 1.43 R\_p; the mass-loss rate falls from 7.3e6 to 2.7e4 g/s (median over
r \textgreater= 1.1 R\_p, x0.004); the He(2\^{}3S) density falls by decades above 1.1 R\_p.
\texttt{src/utils/collisional\_validity.py} on the two states: without conduction
the domain is subsonic (max Mach 0.07) with Kn\_bulk 0.40 at the outer
boundary; WITH conduction the exobase (Kn\_bulk = 1) is at 1.52 R\_p, Kn\_bulk
= 0.1 at 1.30 R\_p, the critical point at 8.8 R\_p, and Kn reaches 859 in the
acceleration region: the hydrodynamic Mdot (log 4.41) is UNVALIDATED, and
Jeans escape through the exobase (T = 932 K, lambda\_J = 17) gives 9.8 g/s.
\emph{Likely} the low-XUV LHS 1140 b atmosphere with conduction is a
conduction-limited thermosphere in the Jeans regime, the structure of the
Earth\textquotesingle s thermosphere, and not a hydrodynamic wind; the continuum solution
above \textasciitilde1.3 R\_p is outside its validity. Not established: the fiducial-XUV
cases with conduction (not yet solved), and the element enthalpy flux,
whose continuation from this state is running.

}

At the fiducial XUV conduction redistributes the heat (the lower layer
warms, the peak cools by 19 per cent) and lowers the mass-loss rate by 31
per cent; the solution stays a subsonic continuum wind with the exobase
above the domain, UNVALIDATED on the same grounds as before (Kn 0.7-1.1 in
the acceleration region, no critical point in the domain). At 0.10 of that
XUV (section 45) the same term turns the wind into a conductive thermosphere
with an exobase at 1.5 R\_p. The effect on the He 10830 equivalent width is
not yet computed.

\subsection{49. The advected temperature correction keeps the transport terms (2026-09-30)}\label{49-the-advected-temperature-correction-keeps-the-transport-terms-2026-09-30}

\textbf{Defect.} The advection-corrected profiles (\texttt{post\_process\_adv}, the
\texttt{*\_adv.txt} files the He 10830 synthesis reads) re-derive the temperature
from the steady internal-energy equation (E), div(u v) + p div v = heating -
cooling, which holds neither heat conduction nor the enthalpy flux of the
element and carrier fluxes. On a run that solved them the corrected
temperature is then the answer to another equation: MEASURED on the
fiducial atomic case on the full equation (section 48), 7939 K against the
run\textquotesingle s 5489 K at 1.2 R\_p and 8479 K against 6259 K at 1.5 R\_p.

\textbf{Fix} (user decision 2026-09-30: the consistent form, conduction of the
corrected temperature). \texttt{T\_equation} takes heating = photoheating +
\texttt{heat\_extra} + \texttt{cond\_diag} T (new \texttt{teq\_state} fields, zero for a run without
the terms: the residual is unchanged); \texttt{post\_process\_adv} fills them from
the conduction triplets of the run\textquotesingle s operator (\texttt{thermal\_conduction\_coeffs},
now public) and the enthalpy flux divergence of the element
(\texttt{interdiffusion\_enthalpy\_divergence\_of\_state}) and carrier
(\texttt{carrier\_enthalpy\_divergence}) fluxes. Conduction couples each cell to both
neighbors, so after the marching sweep (cell below at its corrected value,
cell above at the pass\textquotesingle s profile) the corrected cells are solved together
by a Newton iteration on the column (\texttt{energy\_column\_newton}: banded
Jacobian, two sub- and one super-diagonal, by one-sided differences; the
conductivity and the enthalpy flux divergence re-evaluated at each iterate;
steps capped at 30 per cent; converged at a relative step of 1e-10; a solve
that fails returns the marching profile and is reported). Cells that keep
the run\textquotesingle s temperature are fixed values of the solve.

\textbf{Checks.} gfortran and ifx builds; \texttt{src/tests/adv\_static\_limit}: 53
PASS, 0 FAIL; \texttt{src/tests/energy\_update}: 54 PASS (section 41). A run without
the terms (the fiducial term-off state): the column solve does not run, the
solved state is bitwise unchanged, and the \texttt{*\_adv} rows move by at most
1.4e-11 relative (the ifx build reassociates the heating sum; below the
standing 0.1 per cent rule). The full-equation fiducial state: the column
solve converges in 4 to 7 iterations each pass (relative step 5e-11, 502
cells); the corrected temperature follows the run\textquotesingle s where conduction
carries the energy (1129/5507/6346 K against 1127/5489/6259 K at
1.01/1.2/1.5 R\_p) and departs from it beyond 3 R\_p, where the
non-equilibrium composition changes the balance (figure
\texttt{.P1/atomic97/diag\_R/adv\_transport\_terms\_fiducial.png}).

\textbf{He 10830 EW} (WINERED kernel, the catalog\textquotesingle s operator,
\texttt{.P1/fiducial\_conduction/ew\_of\_state.sh}; observed 1.108 +- 0.029):

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter

\toprule\noalign{}
state & adv & solution (no advection correction) \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
term-off (no conduction, no element enthalpy flux) & 1.573 & 12.99 \\
full equation, adv without the transport terms & 0.946 & 7.52 \\
full equation, adv with them & \textbf{0.649} & 7.52 \\
\end{longtable}
}
}

On this case heat conduction and the element enthalpy flux lower the model
EW from 1.57 to 0.65 (x0.41); the He/H at which the model EW crosses the
observed value (1.5 in the catalog, section 14) will move. The previous EW
of every conduction run is superseded by this operator.

\subsection{50. A floor of the energy row near convergence (2026-09-30; located in section 53, explained and removed in section 58)}\label{50-a-floor-of-the-energy-row-near-convergence-2026-09-30-located-in-section-53-explained-and-removed-in-section-58}

Two chains slow down on rungs whose hydrodynamic solve ends at the
400-iteration cap (info = 1): the 0.0624 conduction rung at factor 0.751
(He/H row 1e-7, energy row 1e-5 to 5e-5 against its tolerance 1e-6 for
twelve passes of 140 s each; the rung then CERTIFIED at pass 15, 15:44, when
one pass\textquotesingle s state fell inside every tolerance) and the first XUV descent rung
of the photochemical case (0.10 -\textgreater{} 0.0833, passes of 900-1000 s, running).
The rungs are not stuck but their certification rests on a fluctuation. In both the solve reaches \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} \textasciitilde{} 5e-5 within
25 iterations and then stays between 5e-6 and 5e-5 for the rest of the cap,
at dtau = 1e14, with every step accepted.

Tried: \texttt{EXHALE\_PTC\_SER\_RNORM} (default 1e-3), the pseudo-time acceptance of
section 42 only while \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} is above the switch and the merit line search
below it (binary \texttt{EXHALE\_1543a8f2.x}). The two fixtures of section 42 still
certify in 22 and 28 iterations (T1 \textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} 3.5e-7, T3 1.5e-7). On the 0.0624
rung 0.579 -\textgreater{} 0.751 (\texttt{.P1/atomic97/diag\_R/ser\_phase/P1\_*}) the transient is
crossed as before (\textbar\textbar R\textbar\textbar{} 1.9 -\textgreater{} 5.7e-5 in iterations 19 to 24) and the
merit phase then wanders at the same 1e-5 to 5e-5 for 376 iterations, the
Krylov forcing term pinned at its ceiling 0.1 (the residual not falling,
the Eisenstat-Walker rule does not tighten it). The stall is therefore not
the acceptance rule. \emph{Likely} the energy row carries a floor of that size on
this state (candidates: the tolerance of the composition elimination inside
the residual, an ionization root switching branch); not measured. The test
was stopped after pass 1. The switch is kept (it changes nothing on the
fixtures and is the standard terminal phase), with this record that it did
not cure the stall.
